Per-service debt + go-lives: paperless, inboxclean, miniflux, mail-relay, immich, forgejo, hermes, bank-sync, browser-history, dnsblockd, discordsync, searxng, CV, attic, signal, monitor365, google-sync, tq pool units, PMA units. (twenty + all Docker-era services removed 2026-10-08.)

Domain LIBRARY of the [TODO system](../../TODO_LIST.md) — every open item for this domain, any lifecycle. The dispatch QUEUE of agent-actionable (`[ready]`) items is `TODO_LIST.md`; the tq pool harvests only the queue.

House rules (enforced by convention, see AGENTS.md → "TODO System"): file an item under the domain that owns the FIX, not the symptom it mentions; one item = one ask + `**Source:**` pointer — verification narratives go to the status report, never the item; `[x]` rows are pruned to `CHANGELOG.md` at every pass; NO system-state narratives here (AGENTS.md and `docs/services/*` runbooks own state).

Tag legend: `[ready]` agent-actionable · `[blocked:user]` needs sudo/browser/external console/owner hands · `[blocked:push]` needs an upstream push/tag (agents implement, never push) · `[blocked:deploy]` waits on a deploy · `[watch]` time-gated verification · `[decision]` owner question.

- [ ] [ready] **Name the backend that compresses media responses** — the 2026-10-06 Caddy encode investigation proved Caddy's innocence (default Content-Type allowlist live-verified on the system 2.11.4 binary: html/json/svg+xml → zstd; png/jpeg/webp/mp4/octet-stream → NOT encoded; runbook section "encode / compression behavior" in docs/services/caddy.md) and identified the mechanism: `reverse_proxy` forwards the client's `Accept-Encoding` unchanged, browsers attach it to every fetch, so backends with compression middleware (NestJS/Express `compression`, Django GZipMiddleware, …) compress media themselves. - [ ] [ready] **dnsblockd readiness gate proves the WRONG port: it polls the block page (:80, TCP) not the DNS contract (:53 UDP+TCP)** — `dnsblockd-wait-listener` (dns-blocker.nix:1346-1367) greps `ss -ltn` output for `:${cfg.blockPort}`; `ss -ltn` is TCP-only and never mentions 53, so the sole-resolver contract is unwatched by its own gate (the 2026-10-12 wedge is caught only incidentally, because it bound NO listener). Preferred fix: `Type = "notify"` (the deployed binary already calls `sdnotify.Ready()` after `srv.Startup()` — cmd/dnsblockd/main.go:137 at lock `f9bb6477`, verified) + `AF_UNIX` added to `RestrictAddressFamilies`, deleting the 300s poll entirely; interim one-liner: `ss -ltnu` + match `:53`; ship a NEGATIVE TEST in the same change (a listener map with only `:80` open must FAIL the new check, so the gate can never silently regress to a proxy port). **Source:** docs/architecture-understanding/2026-10-28_10-18_dnsblockd-integration.html §E6/§E8 (issue #2) — **[x] DONE 2026-10-29 (interim form, in-tree + deploy-gated):** `dnsblockd-wait-listener` now probes `ss -ltnu` for `ports.dns-blocker` (:53) over TCP+UDP with exact suffix matching (`:5355` never matches `:53`); single-transport = WARN-pass (degraded resolver is not dead — restart-churn is the outage class), no listener = start-job FAIL carrying the `startup phase complete` journal discriminator. Negative test: `checks.dnsblockd-readiness-gate` (flake/parts/checks/dnsblockd-guards.nix) runs the REAL rendered script against 5 fixture maps — :80-only FAILs (the old wrong-port proof), :5355-only FAILs, healthy PASSes, udp-only WARN-passes, broken-ss fail-OPENs. `Type="notify"` was considered and deliberately deferred: sd_notify fires after Startup() but the poll's budget coverage of slow blocklist loads (journal-measured 61.5–255.5s) is the harder contract. Verified green 2026-10-29.

Path A (simplest, complete for what Discord still serves): per-channel `POST /api/backfill/restart?channel_id=` — the dashboard's Reset button, needs the API key — with backfillOnStartup temporarily enabled so the bot re-fetches history (idempotent; rate-limited; heavy on media). Path B (surgical; also recovers messages since-DELETED from Discord): stop service → dump live (preserves Oct 14 13:32→now) → integrity_check the quarantine `discordsync.db.corrupt-20261014T010152` → swap it in → backfill only the Oct 14 01:00→now tail. Both need owner sudo/API key; ~5.3 GB quarantine graveyard (4.2 GB Sep 20 + 1.1 GB Oct 14) stays owner-gated until AFTER recovery. **API-key prerequisite RESOLVED (2026-10-15 code-read): NONE** — the auth middleware only installs when `APIKey` is configured (`internal/api/server.go` wraps the handler `if apiKey != ""`), the 2026-09-02 "no auth key configured" note still holds (unauthenticated API reads work), and the listener is loopback-bound — Path A's `POST /api/backfill/restart` works as-is, no key setup step. **RE-ADJUDICATED (2026-10-15, this session):** (1) **Aug 4 = GENUINE corruption, NOT lock-misread** — the Aug-03/04 wdt-crash reports verify the DB was the ONLY corrupt DB among all readable DBs after the hard crash, and the BTRFS snapshots were hours-fresh pre-hot-migration (restore source legitimate); the follow-on readonly crash-loop was the missing chown-back (different defect, since fixed in-script). (3) **Oct 14 = lock-misread, fully proven.** Recurrence story: 1 confirmed + 1 possible + 1 absent — and the deployed controls (lock-guard, hot-tier snapshot refusal, WAL-pair, chown-back) cover BOTH classes regardless. **Source:** 2026-10-15 diagnosis session — dump forensics + heal journal + script read (this row replaces the closed root-cause half of the old 461)

- [ ] [watch] **ADS-1800W USB bus drop-off class (no recovery in-process or in-session)** — the device disappeared mid-scan at 09:32 (kernel released the blocked URB only at 10:01:36 — `usb 6-1: USB disconnect, device number 11`), `timeout` returned rc=124, and the scanner stayed gone until a **physical replug** at 10:50:42 (device number 13). Neither the wrapper nor an agent session can reset the port (rebind/authorized toggle are root-owned, `usbreset` needs root). If it recurs during the campaign, the candidate fix is a root-side recovery unit; the wrapper's rc=124 branch already probes `scanimage -L` and tells the operator to replug. **Source:** this session — `journalctl -k` usb 6-1 events + `pgrep`/`scanimage -L` probes

- [ ] [watch] **ADS-1800W driver-level failure burst: rc=139 SIGSEGV + rc=1 device-open + rc=6 jam (all self-recovered)** — the 2026-10-19 end-of-day sweep of `watch.log` shows the flaky classes beyond rc=4: **332 × rc=4** (seating), **2 × rc=6** (`Batch terminated, 0 pages scanned` — SANE JAMMED), **5 × rc=1** (`scanimage: open of device brother5:bus6;dev1 failed: Invalid argument` — the device could not even be opened), and **1 × rc=139** at 12:15:06 (`timeout: the monitored command dumped core` — scanimage **segfaulted**; no core landed in the repo, and systemd-coredump holds only quickshell cores from 2026-10-18). The burst ran 12:15:06 → 12:15:44 and then cleared by itself: the next polls returned rc=7 (empty ADF) with no replug. So there are at least three distinct device-level failure modes behind "the scanner is being flaky", all of which the WARN text currently explains as if they were rc=4 seating problems. If a burst does NOT self-clear, the recovery is a replug (see the row above); the counting belongs in the runbook so a rising rate is visible rather than absorbed by retries. **Source:** `~/.cache/scan-to-paperless/watch.log`, swept 2026-10-19 23:2x (own session)

- [ ] [ready] **Watch-loop rc explanation must be conditional on the actual code (rc=6 mislabelled as rc=4 in production output)** — the WARN line appends `rc=4 = paper at the feeder with the pick rollers not yet engaged — push the sheet in until it stops` to **every** non-4 failure. Live counter-example in `~/.cache/scan-to-paperless/watch.log`: three consecutive failures at 09:04:28 / 09:04:31 / 09:04:35 on 2026-10-19 carried **rc=6** (SANE `JAMMED`) and were still explained with the rc=4 seating cure — the operator was told to push a sheet while the ADF was jammed. Add the code→action table to `docs/services/scanner.md`. **Source:** docs/status/2026-10-19_11-13_scanner-watch-silent-death-and-loop-hardening.md §d.2/§f.1/§f.23

- [ ] [ready] **monitor365 spawns `dmesg` every 60 s and dies on seccomp (SIGSYS) — one 31 KB coredump per minute** — `dmesg` runs as uid 966 (monitor365) and is killed on `klogctl` (`audit type=1326 … syscall=103 sig=31/SYS`, journal-verified 10:02–10:25 today), so its kernel-message collector can never work under the `harden{}` default empty `CapabilityBoundingSet`. Cost: ~44 MB/day of coredumps in `/var/lib/systemd/coredump`, a per-minute journal storm, and a noise floor that masks real `dmesg` signals in every autopsy (the same loop fired in freeze #33's death minute). - [ ] [decision] **Arm dnsblockd pprof (`services.dns-blocker.pprofEnabled`) for wedge forensics (OWNER)** — landed OFF in plan T06 with a render-test variant. - [ ] [decision] **Host-protection design gap: rpi3-first resolver has no fallback when rpi3 is down — the gateway captures the HOST** — resolv.conf = .151 first / 127.0.0.1 second / 9.9.9.9 third, but the lie answers at 127.0.0.1 instantly so the third nameserver never wins; with rpi3 down the design contract ("keeps git@github.com working") is void. Design row: a host-side exemption (dnsblockd local/self exemption upstream, or a 9.9.9.9-first order for gateway-armed hosts, or an allowlist floor for the box's own by-name deps). **Source:** 2026-11-07_06-25 status report §a/§d

- [ ] [decision] **Operator-side CV credentials-store access (the 0700 identity split)** — the login loop runs as `cv` because the sealed store + master key are `cv`-owned 0700, while the operator's `/home/lars/projects/CV/data/accounts` ledger is a separate lars-owned session store; a cv-refreshed session therefore does not help lars-run timers (gunio-weekly, the A.Team bridge recipes). Options: (a) keep the split and run every credential-consuming automation as `cv` (documented today), (b) grant lars traversal+read on the sealed store dirs (0750 + group `cv`) and feed the master key to lars-run units via a root-only sops template + systemd `EnvironmentFile`, so relogin writes the operator ledger directly — at the cost of a short-lived key window inside a lars-owned process env, (c) wait for the upstream store write API and make the sealed `session_blob` the single source for both identities. **Source:** docs/services/cv.md §Credential storage + CV repo status 2026-11-06 §f.7
